And he revealed to these dervishes the tricks they used to enter the fire, and to make foam come out of their mouths, by simulating epileptic seizures; he said to them: "The individual who wants to enter the fire, let him begin by washing his body entirely in the bath, then, let him rub it with vinegar, after which, let him enter into the flames, and I will enter with him: and thus, if he enters into the flames, they do not surround him; but that is a sleight of hand that is familiar to the mountebanks among us." The prestige of these sorcerers fell before this exposition of Ibn at-Taymiyya; they formed a considerable troop; the session ended for them in such a way that they were gratified with iron collars and that any person who transgressed the precepts taught by the Quran and the Traditions of the Prophet had his head cut off. This year, arrived at the Imperial Gates, coming from the land of the Tatars, the brothers of the Emir Saif ad-Din Salar, Lieutenant-General of the Sultan in the exercise of the august sovereignty, namely Hosam ad-Din Tchébé


[p. 114] {#po.20.p114}
الممظمة وهم حبام الدين حا وفض الدين داوود ووالدتهم فأنعم السلطان عليهما وامرهما طبل خانات© وكان للامير سيف الدين سلار منذ فارقهم.من نونة الابلستين: فى الدولة الظاهريّة فى سنة خمس وسعين وستمائة الى حين اجتماعه ثلشين سنة وكان وصوليم فى شهر صف من هذد السنة« وفيبا طلب الشيخ تقى الدين أبن التيميا من دمشتي الى الابواب الشريفة فحضر وعقد له مجلس بدار نائب السلطنة وجرت مفاوضات كبيرة آخرها ان الشيخ تقى الدين اعتقل فى احد ابراج القلمة© وها وصل رسول الملك المؤبّد صاحب اليمن ومعه البدايا والتقادم من البهار والقنا والاقمشة والتحف زغير ذلك وقول بما جرت به العادةه ذكر نكتة غريبة© وفى هذه السنة ظبر فى معدن الزمود قطعة زمرّدٍ مطاولة بتربيع غشيم زنتها مائتى خسة وارحون مشقالً محزرا وجها الواحد ذيابى والاخر
Fakhr ad-Din Daoud, and their mother. Al-Malik an-Nasir showed them great liberality, and he conferred upon both of them the rank of emir tabl-khana. There had elapsed, from the day when the Emir Saif ad-Din Salar had been separated from them, at the battle of al-Abulustayn, under the reign of al-Malik az-Zahir Rokn ad-Din Baïbars, in the year 675, until the moment when he found them again, a period of thirty years. These personages arrived in Cairo, in the month of Safar of this year 705. This same year, the Sheikh Taki ad-Din ibn at-Taymiyya was summoned from Damascus to the Imperial Gates; he presented himself, and he was made to appear in a session that was held at the palace of the Viceroy; great discussions arose, which ended with the conclusion that the Sheikh Taki ad-Din was incarcerated in one of the towers of the Mountain Fortress. This year, arrived the ambassador of al-Malik al-Mu'ayyad Hizabr ad-Din Daoud, King of Yemen; he brought presents, offerings of spices, of clusters of dates, of muslins, of gifts of all kinds, and other things besides. This tribute was accepted, according to custom. History of a strange adventure. This year, there was discovered in the emerald mine a crystal of this precious stone, elongated, on an irregular, square base, which weighed two hundred and forty-five miskals, without any defect;


[p. 115] {#po.20.p115}
سلقى هكذى ذكر كرحى ابريدى فى العشرين من شال من هذه السنة وعاينها «توجّه الى مدينة قوس فى طلهاه قال غير انّ هذه قطعة الزمرّد المذكورة وقعت لعلم الدين سنجر الزمّدى ضامن معدن الزمرد وأباعها على يد لول الكاك الدلّال بقوس لابن عمانة التاجر الكارمى بسثماثة ديار مصريّة فلمّا شاع خرها طلبوا ابن عقانة بسيبها فأنكرها وتوجّه ها الى اليمن فجابت له ثلاثة الاف ديثار فلم يبيعها8 وورد تيا الى قوص وأخفاها فغمز عليه سنج الزمّدى لما طلب بالحمل وذكر انها رهن عند ابن عّانة على سثمافة دينار فطاب ابن عمانة واشت من وحمت الى الخزانة على يد الامير
one of its faces was of zoubabi emerald, the other of salki emerald. Thus recounted Gurtchi, the postman, on the twentieth day of the month of Shawwal of the said year; he had seen it with his own eyes, for he had gone to Qus to look for it. Another personage said that this emerald crystal of which we have just spoken fell into the possession of 'Alam ad-Din Sindjar az-Zamarroudi, farmer of the emerald mine; he had it sold, through the care of Lu'lu' al-Hakkak, who was a broker in Qus, to Ibn 'Affana, the merchant of Qarem, for the price of six hundred Egyptian dirhams. When the discovery of this emerald was learned (in Cairo), Ibn 'Affana was summoned to the court to buy it from him; but this personage refused to do so, and he left for Yemen, where he was offered three thousand dinars; he did not want to sell it at that price. He returned with this stone to Qus, and hid it. 'Alam ad-Din Sindjar az-Zamarroudi denounced Ibn 'Affana, when he was asked for the amount of his royalty, and he asserted that it was pledged with this personage,
against a sum of six hundred dinars. Ibn 'Affana was brought (to Cairo), and the emerald was taken from him, which was deposited in the treasury, through the care of


[p. 116] {#po.20.p116}
سابق الدين ابورتا! الساقى مع الحمل فأقام ابن عمانة بعدها سبعة ايام ومات مفقوع الفؤاد بسييا» ودخلت سنة سّة وسبع ماثة للبجرة© والخليفة بحاله والملوك والتواب بحليم على ما تقم شرحه فى السنة الخليةه وفى هذ« السنة عزل الصاحب سعد الدين: بن عطايا عن الوزادة .والامير علم الدين سنج ألجاولى عن الاستادداريّة وتولى الوزارة القاضى ضياء الدين النشاءدى وهو فقيه وكان اذذاك الوقت ناظر الدواوين وقها وصل حمل سيس وكان فيه دراهم سلطانية سثماثة الف درهم وقماش ونعال للخيل ومسامير غير تكملة ارين حملا ومائتى وسعين انيرا من المسلمين© وفيا رسم للصاحب تاج الدين
the Emir Sabik ad-Din Abourita as-Saki, with what 'Alam ad-Din had sent as the price of his farm. After these events, Ibn 'Affana survived seven days, then he died, heart-broken at having lost this stone. Year 706 of the Hegira. The Caliph reigned, the kings and the governors of the strongholds, at the beginning of this year, were in the same status as that which was described in the account of the events of the previous year. This year, the Sahib Sa'd ad-Din ibn 'Ataya was removed from the functions of Vizier, and the Emir 'Alam ad-Din Sindjar Altchaouli was dismissed from the office of Grand Master of the Sultan's court. The Kadi Ziya ad-Din an-Nasha'i was invested with the Vizierate; he was a doctor of law, who, at that date, was inspector of the ministries. This same year, arrived the tribute for which the Prince of Sis was liable; it contained, in Sultanian dirhams, the sum of six hundred thousand dirhams, fabrics, horseshoes, nails, and other objects, the whole forming forty loads; at the same time, two hundred and seventy prisoners returned. This same year, a rescript notified the Sahib Tadj ad-Din ibn ash-Shirazi of his appointment to the post of inspector of the administration offices, in Damascus,


[p. 117] {#po.20.p117}
ابن الشيرازى بنظر الدواوين بالشام المحروس واخلع عليه وسفن من الديار المصريّة وفيها عادت رسل السلطان من عند الملك طقطاى وهم الآمير سيف الدين بلبان الصرخدى"- وسيف الدين بلبان الحكيمى وفض الدين محمود امير اخور ستنقر الاشتقر وصحتهم رسول من حهة طقطاى. اسمه نامون فولغ فى اكرامه واعيد بالجواب وسفر معه بدر الدين تكمش! الخزندارى وفض الدين محمور المفكور اعلاهه ذكر لكتة غريبة الم سيمع بمثلباله قال المؤتّخ وفى هذه السنة ورد مطالعة الامير سيف؟ الدين قفجاق المنسورى نائب حماة تتضمّن ان باراضى بارين من عمل حماة حبلين بينهما واد يجرى الماء فيه فانتقل نصف الجبل الواحد من موضمه الى الجبل الاخر والتصق به ولم يسقط فى الوادى الذى بينهما شىء من حجارته© وان النائب بحماة كشف ذلك بالقاضى ببارين
the well-guarded city; he was conferred a robe of honor, and he left Egypt. This year, returned the ambassadors whom the Sultan had sent to King Tokhtai; they were the Emirs Saif ad-Din Balaban as-Sarkhadi, Saif ad-Din Balaban al-Hakimi, and Fakhr ad-Din Mahmoud, who had been a squire of Sonkor al-Ashkar. These personages were accompanied by an ambassador whom Tokhtai had sent at the same time as them, and who was named Namun. He was treated with the greatest marks of honor; after which, he was sent back to the states of his master, with a reply to the letter he had brought. Badr ad-Din Tigmish al-Khazindari and this Fakhr ad-Din Mahmoud, of whom mention has just been made above, set out with him. Account of an extraordinary fact, such as one has never heard the like. The historian said: In this year, arrived a report from the Emir Saif ad-Din Kiftchak al-Mansouri, governor of Hamah, in which it was explained that, in the canton of Barin, which depends administratively on Hamah, there were two mountains separated by a valley, at the bottom of which flowed a river. Half of one of these mountains was found transported from its site to the other mountain, to which it came to attach itself, and not a single one of its stones fell into the valley that ran between them. The Emir, governor at Hamah, had had knowledge of this phenomenon through


[p. 118] {#po.20.p118}
وعمل به محضراً وطول النصف الذى انتقل من الجبل مائة ذراع وعشرة اذرع وعرضه خمس وخمسون ذراعا وسافة الوادى الذى بين الجلين مائة ذراع وقوّنت المطالعة بالمحضر المذكور© وفيها وقعت صاعقة بدمشق فى بستان ابى تروس بوادى باب شرقى بغياض السشرجل وكانوا رجال الغيط قد حرثود ثم جلسوا للغداء واذا بصاعقة قد نزت من السماء لحقت ثلاثنة مر وكان احدهم على رأسه قبع صوف وفيه دراهم فاختلطت بعضها بعض بحيث نقيت سبيكة واحدة والاخر لحقت ساقه وطرف كعبه والثالث كان معظمبا عليه فمات ونزلت فى الارض وسلم باقى الجماعة وتوجّعوا الاثنين ايام ثم صلحواة قال1 المؤتّخ حكى الشيخ معين الدين بن القلانسى قال دخلت الى سقاية بمدرسة نور الدين الشهيد بدمشق وبمى كيس اطلس احس بشرابة حوير اخضر وفيه الف دئار
the Kadi of Barin, who had drawn up a report of what he had observed; the half that had transported itself from the mountain had a length of one hundred and ten cubits, and its width was fifty-five cubits; the width of the valley that separated the two mountains was one hundred cubits. The report that he had drafted was joined to the aforementioned report. This same year, lightning struck in Damascus, in the orchard of Abu-Turus, in the valley of the Eastern Gate, in the quince woods; the gardeners had just been digging the earth there, and they had sat down to have lunch, suddenly, lightning fell from the sky, and struck three people; one of these men was wearing a wool cap on his head, in which he had put some silver coins; these coins were melted and agglomerated with one another, so much so that they formed only a single ingot; the second was struck in the leg, and received a wound to the ankle; as for the third, the greatest part of the violence of the lightning fell upon him, and he died; the lightning fell on the ground, and the rest of the troop escaped safe and sound from this adventure; the two men who had been struck dragged themselves to bed for a few days, then they returned to health. The historian said: The Sheikh Mu'in ad-Din ibn al-Kalanisi said: I entered a courtyard where there was a fountain, at the college of Nur ad-Din, the
Martyr, in Damascus; I had with me a purse of red satin, provided with a green silk cord, in which was a sum of one thousand Egyptian dinars; I placed it in the (niche of an) arcade, and I squatted down to


[p. 119] {#po.20.p119}
مصريّة فوضته فى طاقة وجلست لقضى ضرورتى فلما استقق بى الجلوس وأذا الباب فتح ودخل! اعجتى واخذ الكين من الطاق ققمت اليه وتعلقت به وقات اعطيلى كيسى» وهو يقول أخذت كيسى وقد وضته؟ فى حجرة وضع عليه ثيابه وهو يجرى ونا خلفه فلعًا صرنا فى وسط المدرسة قدّام الفسقيّة وكان الشيخ حمال الدين الحصيرى قد حض لالقاء الدرس فأمر الفقهاء باحضارنا اليه فحضرنا بين يديه فقال ما حديثكما فأخرنه بتضيتى فتمال للعجمى ما حديثك أنت فقال دخات أنا قبله الى هذا البيت ومعى "الف دنار مصرتّة فى كيس اطلس احم بشزابة حرير اخضر وانسيتها مع ذكرتها وجت حتّى اخذها فقام هذا تعلق بى فقال له الشبخ انفض حجرك واكشف الكيس حتّى يتبيّن لنا الحقّ مع من هوه فنفض حجر فوقع منه كيسين احمرين اطلس شراريب حرير أخضر وفى كل واحد منهم الف دينار وكان كيسى مكتوب عليه اسمى فنائله الشيخ
satisfy a need; hardly had I begun to engage in this occupation, when the door opened, and a Persian entered, who took the purse from the niche where I had placed it; I got up, I ran to him, I grabbed him, and I said to him: "Give me my purse!" But the individual replied to me: "I took my purse that I had put in a niche"; he tightened his clothes around him, and went away, while I pursued him. When we had thus reached the middle of the college, in front of the basin where a jet of water gushed, the Sheikh Djamal ad-Din al-Hasiri had just arrived to give his course. He ordered the students of law to bring us before him; we went into his presence, and he said to us: "What do you have to say?" I told him my adventure. "What do you have to answer, you?" he said to the Persian. "I had entered this building before this man," he replied; "I had in my possession a sum of one thousand Egyptian dinars in a purse of red silk, which I was holding by a green silk cord. (I placed it in a niche in the wall), and I forgot it completely; then, its memory returned to my mind, and I returned to take it. Then, this individual rushed at me, and grabbed me by the body. - Go then and search your niche," replied the Sheikh, "and examine the purse, so that the truth may shine before our eyes, and that we may know what to think of it." The Persian went off to probe his hiding place, from which came out two red purses, in satin, with green silk cords, in each of which were one thousand dinars; my name was written on the purse that belonged to me; the


[p. 120] {#po.20.p120}
جمال .الدين لى وناول للعجتىق!كيسه وقال كل واحد منكما صادق فيما ادعالا وكان هذا من المصادفات والاتفاقات العجيب وقوعها واللّه اعلم بذلك©؟ .وفببا توتّى الامين بدر الدين بكتاش3 الفخرى امير سلاح وكان امير كبيراً ليلا "وذلك فى شهر ريع الاخر من هذه السنة وفيا توقى_لبن علاء الدين ابن النحخاس الحلبى المعروف بابن عمرون وحكى العدل شمس الدين ابن المناديلى. تقال اشترى ابن عمرون م عشرة ثياب اطلس ووضعبا فى مكبس واسلبا الى بيته وكان شخين عيّار واقف ينظر اليه فرصدة الى ثانى يوم وقصد دار ليسمع كلامه» فقال ابن عمرون لغلامه خذ هذا الخروف واعمله شوى فعند ذلك اسرع العتّار واشترى خروفً يشابه ذلك الخروف وعمله شوى واحض الى بيت ابن عمرون وقال لاهل البيت سيّدى يقول ان غلامه فلان بثه فى شغل هذا
Sheikh gave it to me, and he handed his to the Persian, saying to us: "Each of you two has been sincere in his claims." This was one of the most surprising coincidences and adventures that one could imagine. Allah alone knows what one must think of this story. This same year, died the Emir Badr ad-Din Bektash al-Fakhri, emir silah; he was a great emir, and a very generous man; this, in the month of Rabi' II of this year. This same year, died Ibn 'Ala ad-Din ibn an-Nahhas al-Halabi, known by the name of Ibn 'Amrun; the clerk Shams ad-Din ibn al-Manadili recounted the following; he said: Ibn 'Amrun, one time, bought ten satin garments, which he had arranged in a trunk, and which he sent to his home. A crook was attending the purchase, and was watching him do it; he watched him until the next day, and he approached his door, to hear what he was saying. Ibn 'Amrun said to his servant: "Take this lamb for me, and have it roasted." The rogue, immediately, set out on the campaign, without losing a minute; he bought a lamb that looked exactly like that of Ibn 'Amrun; he put it on the spit, and he carried it to the house of this personage; he said to the servants: "My master says that he has sent his servant, such and such, somewhere, to do such a thing; here is the sheep that he has prepared;


[p. 121] {#po.20.p121}
الخروف الذى عمله واعطولا الثياب الاطلس. الذى ف المكبس وعدتهم عشرة وهم الذى ادسلهم امس فى الوقت الفلانى© فقالوا له الغلامة صحيحة ولكنّا لا نسلّم اليك اثياب فذهب العتّار الى دكان .ابن عمرون وهو فى بر حسنة وحادثه وباسطه وقال له يا سيّدى من صاغ خاتمك هذا فأتنى رأيته صياغة حسنة واشتبى أعمل مثله فقال له ابن عمرون فلان الصائغ فاخرج العتّار من جيبه صرّةٌ فيها ذهب واخرج منها خمسة دنانير وقال لابن عمرون يا ستدى هؤلاى رهن على هذا الخاتم الى ان امضى به الى الصائغ واعمل مثله واعيده فدفع اليه الخاتم فأخذد ومضى .الى دار أبن عمرون وقال لهم سّدى يقول لكم هذا خاتمه والغلام ارسله فى شغل والسلطان فى الخزانة وقد طلب منه العشرة ثاب الاطلس البذى فى المكبس وهو مستعجل© فقالوا الغلامة صحيحة والخاتم فهو له غير أنّا لا تلم لمن لا نعرفه شين فال ليم اعيدوا لي الخاتم حتى اعيده اليه فقالوا
give him the satin garments that are in the trunk, to the number of ten, those that he sent yesterday, at such an hour!" The servants replied to him: "That is correct, but we will not give you, despite that, the satin garments." The scoundrel went off at once to the shop of Ibn 'Amrun, which was magnificently kept; he entered into conversation with him, chatted at length, and ended by saying to him: "O my master! who made you your seal that I see there on your finger? It is truly an admirable jewel, and I want to have one made for me that is exactly similar. - It is," replied Ibn 'Amrun, "such and such an engraver." The crook pulled from his pocket a purse in which were gold coins, and he took out five dinars, saying to Ibn 'Amrun: "O my master! These gold coins are a pledge for this seal, until I take it to this jeweler, and have one made that is exactly the same; then I will bring it back to you." Ibn 'Amrun handed him the seal; he took it, he went to the house of Ibn 'Amrun, and he said to the service people: "My master tells you that here is his seal, that he has charged the servant that I am with a certain commission; the Sultan is in the Treasury, he has asked him for the ten satin garments, which are in the trunk, and he is extremely pressed for them." The servants replied to him: "That is true, the seal is indeed his; but, despite that, we will entrust absolutely nothing to an individual whom we do not know. - Give me back the seal," he said, "so that I may go and
take it back to him. - We will not give the seal either," replied the servants. The crook insisted strongly, and spent his speeches without counting; the debate threatened to drag on, so much so that the people closed


[p. 122] {#po.20.p122}
ولا الخاتم فألج عليهم بالكلام وطال الشرح وغلّقوا الباب وخشى ان يحض احد من غلمان ابن عمرزون فيسكه وفتضح نهرب© واتا ابن عمزون فانه حضض عند -المساء الى منزله فاحضروا اليه رأسين شوى فقال لهم من أين لنا هذا الرأس الاخر فقالوا له من الذى حاب خاتمك وطلب العشرة ثياب الاطلس الذى سيّرتها امن فقال لهم اعطيتموة اتاها فقيل له نعم© فشرع يخاصم فقالوا له الذنب لك ع أنهم عوفوه صورة ما وقم ففرح بذلك وقال ما نأكل اليلة آلا شوى. الحتّار فقد كسنا الشوى والذهب وقيل كان زنة الخمسة دنانير عشرة مثاقيل وهذا من حملة سعادة ابن عمرون وما حرياته8 وفيا وثى فارس الدين محمد الردّادى احد امراء الطبل خاناة بدمشق وكان هذا فارس الدين فى مبدى أمره من جملة مقدمين الحلقة وكان يتمصخ للامراة الكبار وكانوا يحتونه وصفعوا
the door in his face. He was afraid that one of Ibn 'Amrun's servants would arrive from his shop at the house, that he would take him by the collar, and that his knavery would be discovered, so, he hastened to flee. As for Ibn 'Amrun, he returned home in the evening, and the servants presented him with the two roasted sheep. "But," he said to them, "where does this second beast come from? - From the one who presented himself with your seal," replied these people to him, "and who asked for the ten satin garments that you sent yesterday. - And you gave them to him?" he said. - "Certainly," they replied. Ibn 'Amrun began to make a scene with them: "It is indeed your fault," they said to him, then they told him how the affair had really happened, which pleased him very much: "We will not eat anything tonight," he said to them, "other than the rogue's roast; here we have gained the roast, and the gold coins." It was recounted that the weight of the five dinars made ten miskals; this is one of the numerous traits of Ibn 'Amrun's luck, and of the adventures that befell him. This same year, died Faris ad-Din Mohammad ar-Raddadi, who was one of the emir tabl-khana in Damascus. This Faris ad-Din, at the beginning of his career, was one of the senior officers of the guard; he liked to amuse the division generals with his buffoonery; they liked him a lot, and they would give him shoves, while he said to them: "It is not so, I absolutely must become an emir tabl-khana!" The emirs would burst out laughing at these pretensions.


[p. 123] {#po.20.p123}
وهو يقول لبم لا ب ما اصير امير طبل خاناة فيضحكون من ذلك» كان صديق الامير حسام الدين لاحين نائب السلطنة بالشآم فلمّا تسلطن حسام الدين لاجين المذكور الملب بالملك المنصور سافر فارس الدين من دمشق اليه فلمًا دخل عليه مسك عليه الناموس ليلا وطال قيامه بين يديه فالتفت اليه وقال له قد اعطاك الله ملك مصن والشآم غير تعب أيش بقيت تريد يكتب لك قبراطين فى السماءه فضحك حينئذ السلطان لاجين والامراء ومزحوا معه على العادة مع اقره طبل خاناة ولم يزل على امرته الى ان مات وحكى عنه الامير حمال الدين اقوش الافرم انه اخبرة قبل موته مام انه رأى رؤيا ان قائلًا يقول له قد غفر الله لك ففرح له" نائب الللطنة بذلك© وفها توقى جمال الدين ابو اسحق ابرهيم المعروف بابن السواملى وكان تاحراً كبيراً كثير المال والمعادة وحضّل فى بلاد النتار من المال والمعادة ما لا يوصف© متا حكى عنه انه
This personage was in relation with the Emir Hosam ad-Din Latchin, Viceroy of Damascus. When this Hosam ad-Din Latchin, of whom we have just spoken, became Sultan, and was surnamed al-Malik al-Mansour, Faris ad-Din left Damascus to go to his court; when he had entered his palace, the officer on duty held him by the hands for a certain time; he remained standing for a long time before the Sultan, who ended by turning toward him. He said to him: "Here is that Allah has given you the sovereignty of Egypt and Syria, without it having cost you much trouble; what more do you desire? Do you wish, by chance, that he write two sous to your account in heaven?" This made Sultan Latchin and the generals laugh, who began to joke with him, according to their habit; then Latchin conferred upon him the office of emir tabl-khana, which he did not cease to exercise until the moment of his death. The Emir Djamal ad-Din Oughoush al-Afram recounted regarding Faris ad-Din that, a few days before he rendered his soul, this officer had told him that he had had a vision, during which an apparition had said to him: "Allah has granted you his pardon!" Al-Afram expressed all his satisfaction to him. This same year, died Djamal ad-Din Abu Ishak Ibrahim, surnamed Ibn as-Sawamili; he was a great merchant, who possessed a considerable fortune and many goods. He earned, in money and in properties, in the kingdom of the Tatars, sums that it is impossible to count. Among the anecdotes that are cited on his account, there is this one: he


[p. 124] {#po.20.p124}
اشتزى صدفة مجوفة بدراهم لطيفة ع وضعها على السندان الحديد! وضريها بالمطرقة ليكسرها فخرجالقشر الال وطلع الثانى دزة بيضا مدوّة زنتها خسة عش حبّة فقيل انها ققنت له على الملك ابغا بمبلغ سين الف دينار وكات اول سعادته وكان كبير الصدقات وكان اقل عطاياه خمسين دنار
bought one day a shell presenting a cavity for a sum of a few dirhams; he placed it on an iron anvil, and struck it with a hammer, with the intention of breaking it; the first blow made its calcareous shell fall, the second brought to light a pearl of perfect roundness, weighing fifteen grains. It is said that it was bought from him for a sum of sixty thousand dinars by King Abagha; that was the beginning of his fortune. This Djamal ad-Din was a man who spread himself in alms; the
ودخات سنة سبع وسعمئة© والخليفة المستكفى بحاله وسلطان الديار المصرئّة والبلاد الشامية الملك الناصر وصاحب بلاد اليمن الملك المؤيّد هزين الدين داوود ابن المظقّر شمس الدين يوسف ابن رسول وصاحبا مكة الاميرين عر الدين حميضة وشس الدين رميثة ولدى السيّد نجم الدين ابو نمى وصاحب المدينة الشريف ناص الدين منصور ابن عز الدين حمّاز ابن شبيحة© وصاحب الحجم والعراق خرندا ابن ارغون ابن ابغا ابن
least that he gave was fifty dinars. At the beginning of the year 707 the Caliph al-Mustakfi reigned in the same status as in the previous year; the Sultan of the Egyptian lands and the Syrian countries was al-Malik an-Nasir; the sovereign of Yemen, al-Malik al-Mu'ayyad Hizabr ad-Din Daoud, son of al-Malik al-Muzaffar Shams ad-Din Yusuf ibn Rasul; the two lords of Mecca were the two emirs 'Izz ad-Din Humayza and Shams ad-Din Rumaytha, sons of the Sayyid Nadjm ad-Din Abu Nama; the Prince of Medina, the Sharif Nasir ad-Din Mansour ibn 'Izz ad-Din Djammaz ibn Shiha. The sovereign of Persia and Iraq was Khorbanda son of Arghun


[p. 125] {#po.20.p125}
هولاكوا ومن خراسان الى خان بالق الملك قيدو واولاد براق وهو يومئذ يراسل صاحب مصر ونحمل على رأسه سنجقين احدهما للملك الظاهر كان ارسله اليه والاخر للملك
son of Abagha, son of Hulagu; from Khorasan to Khanbaliq reigned Prince Khaidu and the sons of Barakh; at this date, he maintained diplomatic relations with the Sultan of Egypt, and he had carried above his head two standards, one, on which was embroidered the name of al-Malik az-Zahir Rokn


[p. 126] {#po.20.p126}
المنصور قلاوون كان ارسله اليه ايضاه ومن خان لت الى أقصى الصين قاآان بن اقأن
ad-Din Baïbars, who had sent it to him, the other, in the name of al-Malik al-Mansour Qalawun, who had also had it sent to him. From Khanbaliq to the most distant confines of China, reigned the


[p. 127] {#po.20.p127}
بن جنكز خان وقال جنكرى خان بلرآ المهملة ومن الباب الحديد الى بى القفجاق
Ka'an son of the Ka'an, son of Genghis Khan, whom one names Djingri Khan written with the ra, without a point; from the Iron Gate to the land of Kiftchak, from


[p. 128] {#po.20.p128}
وصوداق وخوارزم الى حدّ القسطنطينيّة فى يد المْلك طقطاى واسمه توقتقا ابن متكوتمر بن ساين! خان بن جنكز خان وهو ابن اخو الملك بركةه وساحب الروم السلطان مسعود بن غياث الدين كيخرو ابن الملك ركن الدين السلجوقى والامور بمملكة الروم 'يومئذ لنتّاب النتار وما للسلطان مسعود معهم غير اسم الملطئة: فقط وصاحب مابدين الملك المنصور نجم الدين بن الملك المظقّر قرا ١سلان الارتقى
Sudak, from Khwarizm to the borders of the empire of Constantinople, the sovereignty belonged to King Tokhtai, whose true name was Tokhtogha, son of Mangu Temur, son of the Sain Khan, son of Genghis Khan; he was the son of the brother of King Berke. The Sultan of the country of Rum was Sultan Mas'ud, son of Ghiyas ad-Din Kai-Khusraw, son of King Rokn ad-Din, the Seljuk; at this date, the affairs of the country of Rum were entirely in the hands of the governors of the Tatars, and Sultan Mas'ud exercised no authority there: he had, by their doing, only the title of Sultan, and nothing more; the Prince of Mardin was al-Malik al-Mansour Nadjm ad-Din son of al-Malik al-Muzaffar Kara Raslan, the Artuqid; the sovereign of the Maghreb was Abu Ya'qub Yusuf, the Marinid.


[p. 129] {#po.20.p129}
وصاحب الغرب ابو يعقوب يوسف المرنى© ونائب الملطنة بمصر الامير سيف الدين سلار ونائب السلطنة بالشام الامير حمال الدين اقوش الافرم ونائب السلطنة بحلب الامير شمس الدين قرا سنقر ونائب السلطنة بطرابلس الامين سيف الدين اسندمر ونائب السلطنة بصفد الامير شمس الدين سنقر شلا المنصورى© ذكر الوحشة الواقعة بين السلطان الملك الناصر والامرا© قال المؤتّخ وفى ال المحم ظهرت الوحشة بين السلطان والامير سيف الدين سلار نائب السلطنة وكذلك الامير ركن الدين بيبرس. الجاشتكين. أستاد الدار وكان السلطان قد امتنع من العلائم اتام وظتٌ الناس انّ ذلك لمرض اعتراه ع دخلا الاميرين الى السلطان فى ثالث الشهر فأنكن عليهما وستهماه فاستعطفاد ولانا له فى الكلام وقالا له نحن مماليك السلطان ومماليك والده السلطان الشهيد وامور كثير مما استعطفا به خاطرة فظع عليهما وعلى الامير سيف الدين بكتن الجوكتدار امير جاندار
The viceroy in Egypt was the Emir Saif ad-Din Salar; the viceroy of Syria, the Emir Djamal ad-Din Oughoush al-Afram; the governor of Aleppo, the Emir Shams ad-Din Karasonkor; the governor at Tripoli, the Emir Saif ad-Din Esendemur; the governor at Safad, the Emir Shams ad-Din Sonkorshah al-Mansouri. Account of the conflict that broke out between the Sultan al-Malik an-Nasir and the emirs. The historian said: On the first day of the month of Muharram, dissension arose between the Sultan, the Emir Saif ad-Din Salar, the viceroy, and also the Emir Rokn ad-Din Baibars al-Tchashniguir, grand master of the palace. For several days, the Sultan had refused to sign the rescripts, and the court had imagined that the cause was an illness that had afflicted him. Finally, on the third day of the month, the two emirs entered the Sultan's presence; the prince would not listen to them and covered them with insults. The two generals tried to bring him back to more conciliatory sentiments, [and] held a speech full of gentleness for him: "We are," they said to him, "the Mamluks of the Sultan, just as we were the Mamluks of his father, the martyr Sultan," and they added a great number of reasons, which won over the prince's mind, so much so that he decorated them with robes of honor, at the same time as the Emir Saif ad-Din Bektemur, the djoukandar,


[p. 130] {#po.20.p130}
وخرحوا من عند فلمًا صاروا بظاهر باب الله قوت نفوسهم وشرعا فى أظبار ما عندهم وتركا باب القلعة فى تلك الليلة مفتوح الاقفال ورسما. بأن تركب جماعة من العسكر تحت القلعة فركب الامير شمس الدين سنقر الاعسر فى جماعة من مماليكه بعد عشاء الاخزة مظبرين السلاح وشقّ المدينة وخرج من باب زويلة الى تحت القلعة وكان قل ذلك قد انقطع فى داره وادعى الضف فلما كانت هذه الفتنة كان اول من ركب وكان ممن ركب ايض اخوة الامير سيف الدين سلاره فخرج اليهم بعض المماليك السلطانّة الاوشاقّة من الاسطبل فراسلوهم بالسهام ورمى الاهير سيف الدين سموك
grand equerry. These personages left the prince's audience; when they had reached outside the Bab al-Koulla gate, their spirits emboldened, and they decided to show the intentions that animated them. Saif ad-Din Salar and Rokn ad-Din Baibars left the locks of the Citadel Gate open during that night, and they promulgated the order for a part of the army to mount their horses to stand below the Mountain Fortress. The Emir Shams ad-Din Sonkor al-A'sar got into the saddle at the head of a certain number of his Mamluks at the very end of the evening, sword drawn; he crossed the city in this guise and went out through the Zawila Gate to go take up a position below the Fortress. Some time before, this officer had retired to his hotel without wanting to leave it, pretending that he was sick; when the hour of this sedition arrived, he was the first to mount his horse; among the generals who did the same were the brothers of the Emir Saif ad-Din Salar. A troop of Sultanic Mamluks, pages of the chamber, came out of the
cavalry quarter and began to shoot arrows at them. The Emir Saif ad-Din Sumuk, brother of Salar, was struck by an arrow; this officer went


[p. 131] {#po.20.p131}
اخو سلار بسهم فوصل الى الثبّاك الذى يجلس فيه السلطان فشقّ ذلك على السلطان
before the latticed tribune in which the Sultan was staying; this event deeply impressed the sovereign and seemed to him a very bad omen. The


[p. 132] {#po.20.p132}
كبر لديه وبات الامرآء الاكابر فى تلك الليلة على مساطب الدركاة بباب_القلّة ملازمين ولا فتع باب القلّة وقف لمامه مماليك الامرآ الاكابر.وهم مكنّزين! سبام فى قم ونوا انّ المماليك السلطانيّة يخرجون عليم اذا فتح الباب© فلم يقع ذلك فصرف الامراء اكثر مماليكهم وجلسوا بالتركاة وتروّدت الرسائل بينهم وين السلطان على لسان الامير جمال الدين اقوش الموصلى تال السبع وغيره ممع سألوا رضى السلطان والتمسوا منه تسليم بعض الخاشّكتئّة لاخماد هذ الفتنة اثائرة فيّرهم الهم بعد مراجعات كثيرة وايمان انهم لا يناليم من. الامرا اذى© وهم سيف الدين تيبغا التركمانى وكان من اخ
greatest emirs kept watch that night on the stone benches surrounding the portico of the Fortress at the Bab al-Koulla gate, waiting for how events would turn. When the Bab al-Koulla gate opened, the Mamluks of these great emirs lined up before it, loading their arrows onto their bows; they thought, in fact, that the Sultanic Mamluks were going to fall upon them as soon as the gate was opened. But this prediction did not come to pass, so much so that the emirs sent back most of their Mamluks while they themselves remained seated under the portico; negotiations began between these officers and the Sultan al-Malik an-Nasir through the mediation of the Emir Djamal ad-Din Oughoush al-Mausili Kattal as-Saba' and other persons. Then, they demanded that the Sultan subscribe to their claims, and they enjoined him to deliver to them a certain number of his private Mamluks to put an end to this revolt that threatened the security of his throne. The prince sent them these personages after much coming and going, and when the emirs had pledged by oath that no harm would come to them from their hands. These Mamluks (thus sent as hostages) were Saif ad-Din Taiboukha at-Turkomani, one of the prince's most intimate familiars, who


[p. 133] {#po.20.p133}
الناس بالسلطان واقريهم عنده وسيف الدين خاص ترك وسيف الدين الحاج بيدمر فسقّروهم الى القدس الشريف للوقت ممع دخلوا الامراء الى الخدمة على عادتهم وضدت هذه الفننة© قال المؤتّخ ولمّا اتصل خبن هذا الحديثة بالامير حمال الدين اقوش الافرم نائب السلطنة بالشام المحروس كتب الى الامرآة يلتزبهم وسّفهم على ما وقع من اخراج هؤلاى المماليك ولتمس اعادتهم وقسم انهم متى لم يعادوا الى خدمة السلطان حض هو م8 وكتب الى السلطان مطالعة يقول انّ المملوك!1 بلغه ان الخواطر الشريفة تتغيّرت على فلان وفلان والمملوك! يسأل عود العواطف الشريفة عليهم وشموليم بالمراحم السلطانيّة واعادتهم الى الخدمة الشريفة ولعًا وصل كتاب. نائب الشكم الى الامرآ بذلك سألوا السلطان فى اعادة
enjoyed the greatest favor in his person, Saif ad-Din Khas Turk, [and] Saif ad-Din al-Hadjdj Baidemur. Immediately, the conspirators made them leave for Jerusalem, the holy city. When this was accomplished, the emirs entered the palace to pay their respects to the Sultan, and this sedition was thus appeased. The historian said: When the Emir Djamal ad-Din Oughoush al-Afram, governor of Damascus, the well-guarded city, received the news of this revolt, he wrote to the emirs to blame them for their conduct, to address the most violent reproaches to them for the way they had behaved toward these Mamluks by deporting them to Jerusalem, and urged them to have them return to Egypt; he swore that if they were not brought back to the Sultan's court, he would arrive with them (in Cairo). At the same time, he wrote a memorandum to the sovereign in which he said: "Your Mamluk has learned that the august spirits of Your Majesty have turned away from such and such; your Mamluk asks as a favor that they regain the imperial favors, that they have their share, like the others, of the attentions
of the Sultan, and that they be made to return to the august service." When the letter from the viceroy dealing with these questions reached the emirs, they asked the Sultan


[p. 134] {#po.20.p134}
المماليك المذكورين فرسم باعادتهم فعادواه وفيها فى خامس عشر المحرّم بعد اخراج المماليك! المذكورين اعلاد رسم باخراج الامير سيف الدين بكتمس الجوكندان. وكان امير جاندار الى الشآم بغين اقطاع وتولى مكانه الامير بدر الذين بكتوت الفتّاح فاتا بكتمر الجوكندار فلمّا وصل الى غرّةٍ عيّنت له الصبيبة فتوجّه اليها واستوخمباه ثم كتب الى الابواب الشريفة وسأل النقلة الى غيرها فعيّن له صرخد ثم انفقت وفاة الامير شمس الدين ستقرشاد المنصورى نائب صفد فى شعان من هذه السنة فكتب للامير سيف الدين بكتم الجوكتدار منشوراً بذلك ونوجّه البهاه وفيا فى ليلة الخميس التاسع من شهر صفر حصل بالديار المصريّة زازلة ولم يطل مكنها» ذكر الوقعة الكائنة بين التتار وبين
to have the aforementioned Mamluks return to Egypt; a rescript was issued ordering their recall, and they returned. In this year, on the fifteenth day of the month of Muharram, after the transfer of the Mamluks mentioned above, it was decreed to banish the Emir Saif ad-Din Bektemur, the djoukandar, who was Emir Grand Equerry, and to relegate him to Syria without assigning him any appanage; the Emir Badr ad-Din Bektout al-Fattah was appointed in his place. As for Bektemur, the djoukandar, when he had arrived at Gaza, he was assigned the government of as-Soubaiba; he went to that place, but he became disgusted with residing there. He then wrote to the Imperial Gates to solicit his change and to ask that he be sent to another place; he was appointed to Sarkhad; it then happened that the Emir Shams ad-Din Sonkorshah al-Mansouri, governor of Safad, died in the month of Sha'ban of this year; a diploma was then written conferring the command of that fortress upon the Emir Saif ad-Din Bektemur, the djoukandar, and he went there. This year, during the night of Thursday, the ninth day of the month of Safar, an earthquake occurred in the Egyptian empire, but it did not last
very long. History of the conflict that broke out between the Tatars and the inhabitants of Gilan


[p. 135] {#po.20.p135}
الكيلانتين فى هذه السنة» قال المؤرّخ انّ الملك خريندة طلب من اهل كيلان فتح طريق من عندهم الى بلادة ومنها الى خراسان لسرعة الاخبار الواردة والصادرة وفى فتح هذه الطريق مضة علييم فامتنعوا من ذلك فأمن الملك خرندا بغزوهم وجهّز الهم سين الف فارس مع مقدّمين © احدهما نائبه قطلوشاه وصحبته ارعون الف والاخر جوبان وصحته عشرون الف ووصلوا الى كيلان ونزل قطاوشاه واصحابه فى صحراء وانفرد جوبان واصحابه فى مكان آض ففتح الكيلانتيون اماكن من البحر فى الليل فوصل العاء الى الجيش ورموا ايض نار فى اشجار واحطاب© فاضريت النار بالقرب منهم والماء صار يزيد عليهم حتى كاد يغرقهم وحاروا فى امرجم ليلا واحاط الكيلانتون بهم يصرخون علهم فقتل
during this year. The historian said: King Khorbanda asked the inhabitants of Gilan to leave free a path that led from their home into his states, and from his kingdom to Khorasan, to accelerate the arrival of news, both of that which came from Khorasan and that which left (from Tauris to be transmitted there). The opening of this road caused great damage to the inhabitants of Gilan, who refused to accede to this request. King Khorbanda then ordered an expedition to be made to reduce them to obedience, and he sent against them an army of sixty thousand cavalrymen under the command of two generals. One of them was his viceroy, Kutlushah, who had forty thousand cavalrymen under his orders; the second, Choban, who commanded twenty thousand men. The Mongol armies arrived in Gilan, and Kutlushah camped with his troops in a plain, while Choban, with his soldiers, separated from him to establish himself in another locality. During the night, the Gilanis made breaches in several points of the seashore, so much so that the waves reached the positions where the Mongol army was standing, at the same time as they threw fire on the trees and on the piles of cut wood. The fire broke out very close to the Tatar camp, while the water rose to the point that it almost submerged them; they lost their heads in the shadows of the night, while the Gilanis surrounded them, shouting wild clamors against them. The greater part of the army was annihilated, and the


[p. 136] {#po.20.p136}
أكثرهم وقتل يعضهم بعضً فى اختلاط الليل وظلمته واصاب خطلوشاة سهمً فمات© وانا اصحاب جوان فلم غللهم ورجعوا .مكسودين واخبر التيّار الغاددة ان الملك جردا كان مع الجيش وانّ بعض الكيلانون اخذه ونجًا به ولا هلك الله اعلم8 وذكروا التجّار ايض ان بلاد كيلان مسيرتها سبعة ايام فى عرض. ثلاثة ايام والبحر محبط ها من جانب والجبال من ناحية اخرى لها طريقان حسب لا غير واراضيهم موحلة تزع فيها! الارز والتوت فالتوت لاجل القت بسبب الحرير والارز لاجل قوتهم وهئ بلاد ضيقة كثيرة التوعر والنذى نه عليهم وسعى فى اذاءهم صاحب مازندران لما بينهم من العداوة والغضاء8 وفيها .وصل
Mongol soldiers killed each other in the confusion created by the night, in the midst of thick darkness. Khutlushah was struck by an arrow and died. As for Choban's troops, the greatest number of his men got out of this bad spot safe and sound, and they returned after having suffered the shame of a complete defeat. The merchants of Baghdad reported that King Khorbanda was with the army, that a Gilani soldier took him prisoner, and that he succeeded in getting out of his hands, otherwise he was a dead man. Allah is the most knowing. These merchants also recounted that the land of Gilan extends over a length of seven days' march, while it is three days in width; the sea surrounds it on one side, the mountains on the other; it is crossed by two paths, and that is all. The lands of these people are swampy lowlands where rice and mulberry trees are cultivated: the mulberry tree for raw silk, which is used for the making of silks; the rice for their food. It is a narrow region whose soil is covered with rocks; it was the prince of Mazandaran who attracted
the attention of the King of Persia to the inhabitants of the region; he did everything in his power to harm them because of the hostility and hatred that reigned between him and these people. This year, news was received that Khorbanda, King of the Tatars, had


[p. 137] {#po.20.p137}
ابض ان خريندا ملك التتار قتل بولاى وانه اسل الى اهل كيلان الشيخ براق فانم قتلود فلمًا سمع خرندا بذلك غضب غضًً شديداً وجهّز اليهم ماثة الف فارس واقام بالقرب منهم والله اعلم» وفيها فى اوائل شهر ربيع الال وصل الامير حسام الدين منّا ابن شرف الدين عيسى بن منًا واجتمع بالسلطان فاكرمه وخلع عليه وخاطب السلطان فى امن الشيخ نقى الدين بن التيميا فاجاب سؤاله فيه وحض الامير حسام الدين بنفه الى الجن الى الشيخ واخرجه وذلك فى يوم الجمعة الثالث والعشرين من ربيع الاقل© مع عقد للشيخ تنقى الدين مجلس حفلًا وجرت ماحث كبيرة اقتضى آخرها الاتفاق على
put Bulai to death, that he had sent the Shaikh Barakh to the inhabitants of Gilan, and that these people had assassinated him. When Khorbanda was informed of this fact, he fell into a terrible rage, and he sent against them an army of one hundred thousand cavalrymen; he remained in junction with it. Allah is the most knowing. This same year, in the first ten days of the month of Rabi' al-Awwal, the Emir Hosam ad-Din Mohanna, son of Sharaf ad-Din Isa ibn Mohanna, arrived; he obtained an audience with the Sultan, who showed him the most flattering marks of his consideration and who gave him a robe of honor. The Emir spoke with the prince about the case of the Shaikh Taki ad-Din ibn at-Taymiyya, and the prince agreed to the intercession he made in his favor. The Emir went in person to the dungeon where the Shaikh was detained, and he gave him his freedom on Friday, the twenty-third day of the month of Rabi' al-Awwal. The Shaikh Taki ad-Din was then brought to appear in a session attended by a great number of people, during which he committed himself
to a great discussion on the charges that were brought against him; it ended with his promise to change the language he held on the articles of dogma, and this session thus ended in good conditions.


[p. 138] {#po.20.p138}
تغبير الفاظ فى العقيدة وانفصل المجلس على خير© وفيها جرّد من دمشق حيثاً كثيف الى الرحبة بسبب العدق المخذول وفيها ورد -الخبى بوفاة ابى يحقوب يوسف المرينى صاخب المغرب وقام بعد ولد ولده صالح© وفيها حصل عزم الامرا على قصد بلاد اليمن وتجريد الساكر وعمارة مراكب لحمل الزاد ثم سأل اعيان الكمارمّة المراحم السلطانيّة فى! تأخير الحركة الى ان يتوجّه رسل من الابواب الشريفة الى صاحب اليمن وود بالجواب فاجاب السلطان سؤالهم وجهّز الرسل وسفروا الى اليمن© وقيها امر الامير ركن الدين بيبرس الجاشنكير استاد الدار العالية بعمارة دار الوزارة التى داخل باب النصل خائقاة ورباط وترية لدفنه
This year, a numerous army was sent from Damascus toward ar-Rahba to go fight against the enemy, whose plans Allah rendered vain. The death of Abu Ya'qub Yusuf, the Marinid, sovereign of the Maghreb, was learned; he was succeeded by the son of his son, Salih. This same year, the emirs resolved to make war on the kingdom of Yemen; they decided to mobilize soldiers and to have ships built to transport supplies. The notables of the merchants of Karem implored imperial favors to delay the troop convoys until the ambassadors who would be sent from the August Gates to the King of Yemen returned, bringing back the response that would be demanded of this prince. The Sultan acceded to their requests; ambassadors were sent, and they set out on the road to go to Yemen. This year, the Emir Rokn ad-Din Baibars al-Tchashniguir, Grand Marshal of the August Palace, ordered that there be built, in the place and stead of the Vizierate Palace which was located inside the Gate of Victory, a


[p. 139] {#po.20.p139}
واهتع بذلك اهتمامً عظيما وغرم علبها اموالاً جع واوقف عليها الاوقاف العظيمة ومد هذا لم تكن من سهمه ولم يذكر فيها اسمه وفاقت ايَامًا مع فتحت© وفها توقى الامير يكن الدين بيبرس المجمى المعروف بالجالق بظاهر الريلة نمع حمل الى القدس الشريف ودفن فيه وكان اميراً بير من الجمداريّة فى ايام الملك الصالح وهو آخر من بقى
Sufi convent, a caravanserai, and an edifice in which he would be buried. He showed extreme eagerness to see these works finished, and he spent enormous sums of money on them, while he constituted a considerable number of landed properties as mortmain goods for their maintenance. But, after that, he was not rewarded with the fruit of his efforts, and his name was not engraved on the stone there; these buildings were closed for days, then they were reopened. This same year, the Emir Rokn ad-Din Baibars al-Adjami, known by the name of Altchaligh, died outside Ramla; his body was transported to Jerusalem, the holy, and received burial in that city; he was one of the
great emirs of the corps of the djamadars under the reign of al-Malik as-Salih Nadjm ad-Din Ayyub; he was the last of the Mamluks of this prince who remained


[p. 140] {#po.20.p140}
من الصالحيّة وكان بالديار المصرّة مم نقل الى دمشق فى سنة تسعين وسثمائة ولغ من العمر مائة وعشرين سنة© ثم توقَى الامير بياء الدين يعقونا الشهبرزورى" وكان امير كيرا رحمه الله وفيها قتل هيتوم صاحب سيس قتله مقدّم من مقتمى التتار المقيمين يسيس وتولى بعده اخود ليفون والله اعلم»
in this lower world; he served in Egypt, from where he was sent to Damascus in the year 690: he had reached the age of one hundred and twenty years. After him, the Emir Baha ad-Din Ba'kouba ash-Shahrzouri died, who was a great emir; may Allah have mercy on him! In this year, Haithoum, prince of Sis, was assassinated; it was one of the generals of the Tatars who held the garrison in Sis who killed him. After him, his brother, Lifon, was invested with the sovereignty. Allah is the most knowing! At the beginning of the year 708 of the Hegira, the Caliph of the Muslims reigned in the same status as in the previous year; the kings also, if one excepts the prince of the lands of Western Africa, who was dead,
ودخات! سنة ثمان وسبع ماثة للبجرة وخليفة المسلمين بحاله والملوك بحالبم خلا صاحب الغرب فانه توقى وتولى مكانه ولد ولده الصالح والتواب بحالبم خلا صفد فان استفق النائب يا الامير سيف الدين بكتمن الجوكندار وفيها اخرج الامير نجم الدين خض ولد الملك الظاهر من الاعتقال وسكن بمصر بدار الافرم8
and who had had as successor the son of his son, as-Salih; it was the same for the governors of the fortresses, except for Safad, where the Emir Saif ad-Din Bektemur al-Djoukandar was then installed in the capacity of lieutenant-general. This year, the Emir Nadjm ad-Din Khidr, son of al-Malik az-Zahir Rokn ad-Din Baibars al-Bondokdari as-Salihi an-Nadjmi, was released from the detention where he was being held. This personage settled in Misr, in the hotel of Djamal ad-Din Oughoush al-Afram, viceroy of Syria.

### Account of the departure of the Sultan al-Malik an-Nasir for al-Karak, and of the stay he made in that fortress
ذكر توجّه السلطان "الملك الناصر الى الكرك واقامته به قال المؤئيخ وفى هذ السنة اظهر السلطان انه قد عزم
The historian said: In this year, the Sultan declared that he had formed the plan to go make a trip in the august Hijaz, and he ordered the preparation of everything he needed for this


[p. 141] {#po.20.p141}
على الحركة الى الحجاز الشريف وامر بتجهيز ما يحتاج اليه وتجهّز معه جماعة من مماليكه الذين اختارهم وتزز من قلعة الجيل المحروسة يوم الببت رابع وعشرين رمضانة وركب الامرآ؟ فى خدمته وودعو وعادوا واستقل ركابه وعتّد عيد الفطر بالصالحّة ع سار ووصل الى قلعة الكرك فى يوم الاحد العاشر من شتال© ولمّا دخل السلطان ومماليكه على الجسر الخشب! ضعفت اخثشابه ولعا ادخل فرس السلطان يديها من باب القلعة وكات رجليها على الجر سقط الجس ولطف الله تعالى وهمزت الفرس برجليبا فصارت من داخل باب القلعة© وسقط فى الخندق جماعة من الخاشكتيّة وهذا الخندق فمن اعمق الحنادق وابعدها قط بعضهم على بعض فلموا ما خلا نفرين احدهما الامير عر الدين
expedition. A certain number of his Mamluks, whom he had chosen to accompany him, prepared to set out on the road with the prince. Al-Malik an-Nasir left the Mountain Fortress, the well-guarded, on Saturday, the twenty-fourth day of the month of Ramadan. The emirs mounted their horses to accompany him at his departure; then, they took leave of him and returned to Cairo, so much so that the imperial procession remained all alone. The Sultan celebrated the feast of the breaking of the fast at as-Salihiyya; then he set out on the road again, and he arrived at the citadel of al-Karak on Sunday, the tenth day of the month of Shawwal. When the Sultan and his Mamluks had set foot on the drawbridge, its beams gave way; at this moment, the horse of al-Malik an-Nasir had placed its two front feet on the embankment where the gate of the citadel rose, while its two hind feet were still on the bridge; the bridge collapsed; but Allah spread his graces upon the prince; his horse lunged forward with its two hind feet, crossed the gate, and penetrated into the courtyard of the fortress. A certain number of the Mamluks of the Sultan's private service fell into the moat; this moat is one of the deepest and widest that can be imagined; they tumbled one on top of the other, but they all got out of it safe and sound, with the exception of two officers, one of whom was the Emir Izz ad-Din al-Hadjdj Ezedemur, who was the commander of the corps of the djamadars; he broke his spine and remained paralyzed in half of his body.


[p. 142] {#po.20.p142}
الحاج ازدمر رأس نوبة الجمداريّة فانه انقطع نخاعه وطل نصفهة ولا استقى السلطان بقلعة الكرك طلب ورقة .بحاصل خزانة الكرك فكتب له ورقة بملغ مامثى "الف درهم وكان الحاصل اضعاف ذلك وانما كنت هذ الورقة برأي النااب خثية ان السلطان يأخذ ما بالقلعة من الآموال تمع ظهر للسلطان ذلك فاخرج النائب من القلعة وهو الامير جمال الدين اقوش الاشرفى» ممع اعاد الساطان بعض الإثقال الذين كانوا صحته الى الديار المصرّة ومن حملة ذلك سبع مائة هجين وغير ذلك حتّى السناجق السلطانيّة وكتب الى النتراب بالديار المصرّة والبلاد الشامية يعلمهم انه قد استقز بالكرك ونزل عن السلطنة وكان ورود مكاتة السلطان الى الامراة بقلعة الجبل فى يوم الجمعة الشانى والعشرين من شهر شتّال من هذد السنة« الثانى عش من ملوك الترك السلطان الملك المظقّر يكن الدين بيبرس الجاشنكير© قال المؤٌتّخ ولعا ورد كتاب السلطان الملك الناصر من الكرك
When the Sultan had installed himself in the citadel of Karak, he asked that he be given a statement indicating what existed in the treasury of this fortress; a statement was drawn up for him noting a sum of two hundred thousand dirhams, while the valuables deposited there amounted to double this figure; but this document had been written on the advice that the governor of the fortress had given, in the fear that the Sultan would lay his hands on all the money that existed in the citadel; it was this document that was shown to the Sultan; he perceived the stratagem and drove from Karak the governor, who was the Emir Djamal ad-Din Oughoush al-Ashrafi. Then, the Sultan sent back to Egypt a part of the baggage with which he had followed himself, including seven hundred camels and many other things, up to the imperial standards; then, he wrote to the governors of the fortresses in the Egyptian empire and in the provinces of Syria to let them know that he had just installed himself at Karak and that he was renouncing the sovereignty. The letter arrived to the emirs at the Mountain Fortress, the well-guarded, on Friday, the twenty-second day of the month of Shawwal of this year. The twelfth of the kings of the Turks was the Sultan al-Malik al-Muzaffar Rokn ad-Din Baibars al-Tchashniguir. The historian said: When the letter of the Sultan al-Malik an-Nasir arrived from al-Karak, the emirs highest in rank assembled,


[p. 143] {#po.20.p143}
اجتمع اعبان الامراء وتشاوروا وانفق رأنهم على سلطنة الامير ركن الدين المشار اليه نع خاطبود بالسلطئة فى دار الامير سيف الدين سلار نائب السلطنة8 وركب من الدار المذكورة ودخل من باب القلة راكب والامير سيف الدين سلار والامراء مشاة بين .يديه نمع جلس على كرسى المملكة فى .يوم السبت الشالك والعشرين من شهر شتّال سنة ثمان وسع مائة© وحلفوا له الامراء بالديار المصربّة وحلف هو للامير سيف الدين سلار على ان يكون نائب السلطنة ولا يخالفه وانهما متّفقين على مصالح المسلمين© ونوجّه الامير عّ
and held council. Their choice fell unanimously on the sovereignty of the Emir Rokn ad-Din, of whom we have spoken in the course of this chronicle; they then offered him the power in the hotel where the Emir Saif ad-Din Salar, the viceroy, lived. Rokn ad-Din went out on horseback from this hotel, and he penetrated into the Mountain Fortress through the Bab al-Koulla gate, which he crossed on his steed, the Emir Saif ad-Din Salar and the other generals walking before him on foot; he sat on the throne of sovereignty on Saturday, the twenty-third day of the month of Shawwal of the year 708. The emirs who resided in the Egyptian empire lent him an oath of fidelity, while he swore to recognize the Emir Saif ad-Din Salar in the capacity of viceroy, never to do anything that could thwart his designs, that on the contrary, both of them would agree on the measures that would be
best for the good of the Muslims. The Emir Izz ad-Din Aibek al-Baghdadi and the Emir Saif ad-Din Sati left


[p. 144] {#po.20.p144}
الدين ايبك الغدادى والامير سيف الدين ساطى الى الشام المحروس لتحليف من به من النتاب والامر مع دكت الشائر. وذيجنت القاهرة ومصر ايام وعد جلؤس السلطان الملك المظتم" بسنّة ايام رزق ولد ذكر وذكر عن الملك المظفّر انّه لا-عرف مماوك ولا جادية ولا كان له سؤيّة مع تولى الاستاد داريّة الامير سيف الدين برلغى© وفيها فى يوم السبت سابع ذى القعدة ركب السلطان الملك المظتّر وعليه الخاعة الخليفتّة وارراب الدولة واعيانها بين يديه وعليهم الخلع والوزير ضياء الدين النشادى حامل تقليد السلطان من حبة امير المؤمنين المستكفى بالله على رأسه وهو فى كيس اطلس اسود» وقال انّ الخلع التى افيضت على اعيان الدولة وغيرهم كانت الف وبائتى خلعة وهذا لم إسمع بمثله فيما قم من الدول© وفى يوم الاثمنين مستهل ذى الحّة ركب السلطان الملك
for Damascus, the well-guarded city, with the intention of having the oath of allegiance to the new sovereign taken by the governors of the fortresses and by the generals. Then, the drums were beaten, and Cairo and Misr were brilliantly decorated for a few days. Six days after his accession, the Sultan al-Malik al-Muzaffar became the father of a son; it has been said that this prince had never had relations with a Mamluk, with a slave, that he had never had a mistress. Then, the Emir Saif ad-Din Bouroulghi was appointed to the office of Grand Marshal of the Palace. This year, on Saturday, the seventh day of the month of Dhu al-Qi'dah, the Sultan al-Malik al-Muzaffar mounted his horse, adorned with the robes of honor that had been conferred upon him by the Caliph; the members of the government and the great dignitaries of the state walked before him, dressed in robes of honor; the vizier, Ziya ad-Din an-Nashai, carried above his head the diploma of investiture by which the Caliph al-Mustakfi billah had conceded the sovereignty to the Sultan al-Malik al-Muzaffar; this act was enclosed in a black satin sachet. It has been said that the number of robes of honor that were conferred on this occasion to the great officers of the monarchy, without counting the other personages who enjoyed this favor, was twelve hundred; this was a profusion such that one had never heard of a similar prodigality under the previous reigns. On Monday, the first day of the month of Dhu al-Hijjah, the Sultan al-Malik al-Muzaffar went on horseback to the Dome of Victory (Kubbat an-Nasr); then


[p. 145] {#po.20.p145}
المظتّر الى قبّة النص وعاد فى خدمته الامراء والحججاب وغيرهم8. وفى هذه السنة توبى الحكيم الفاضل علم الدين ابن ابى خليقة :رئيس الاطبّة بالديار المصريّة ولبلاد الهأميّة وهو اقل حكيم رنب بدمشق شراب الورد الطرتى فى زمان الملك الظاهر واستمى الى الآن ولم يكن قبل ذلك يعرف بدمشق وكان عزمه ان يرب شراب_العنب فلم يوافقه بدر الدين نحسن ولا اخوه الموفق رحمم الله تعالى© وفيها توق الملك السعود نجم الدين خض ابن السلطان الملك الظار ومن قبله بيوم توتّى ولده وهو آخر اولاد الملك الظاهر وثيها توقى الامين عن الدين ايدمر" الرميدى استاد الدار الامير سيف الدين سلار نائب السلطنة©
he returned to the Mountain Fortress, accompanied by the emirs, the chamberlains, and the other dignitaries, who paraded at his sides. This same year, the eminent practitioner 'Alam ad-Din ibn Khalika, superior general of the physicians in the Egyptian empire and the Syrian countries, died; he was the first therapist who established in Damascus the manufacture of distilled rose syrup at the time of the reign of al-Malik az-Zahir Rokn ad-Din Baibars, and it has continued until our days; it was not known before this date in Damascus; 'Alam ad-Din had had the intention of manufacturing grape syrup, but Badr ad-Din and his brother, al-Muwaffik, did not want to allow him to do so; may Allah grant them his mercy! This year, al-Malik al-Mas'ud Nadjm ad-Din Khidr, son of the Sultan al-Malik az-Zahir Rokn ad-Din Baibars, died; one day before his death, he had lost his son; he was the last son of al-Malik az-Zahir; this year also saw the disappearance of the Emir Izz ad-Din Aidemur ar-Rashidi, Grand Master of the Palace of the Emir Saif ad-Din Salar, viceroy


[p. 146] {#po.20.p146}
ودخلت سنة تيع وستمائة اللبجرةة وخليفة المسلمين بحاله وسلطان الديار المصريّة والبلاد القامية الملك المظقر ركن الدين بيبرس والملوك والنتّاب والوزيا” يحليم8 وفى هذ السنة وصلا الاميرين احمد وطلجة اولاد عميرة من آل فضل وهؤلا» اولاد عّ حسام الدين متا وكان لهم متة بيلادم الشتار وكاتوا يؤذوا المييلمين وقطعوا الطرقات فلا توثي والدهم عادوا الى الطاعة واخذ ليم من السلطان امان واقطاعات وعدّتهم نحو خمية الاي بيت من العرب© وفها سفن الشيخ تنقى الدين ابن التيميّة من القاهرة الى ثغر الإكندريّة ولم يمكن احد من حماعته من السبفر معه ودخل من باب الخوخة الى دار السلطان ونقل ليلا الى برج فى شرقى البلد احسن الله خلاصه» ونيها ول الامير علاء الدين التليلى وعلاه الدين الخوارزمى ووصل معيم الشيخ ابو يحبى زكريا اللحيانى نانب
At the beginning of the year 709 of the Hegira, the Caliph of the Muslims was al-Mustakfi billah, as in the previous year; the Sultan of the Egyptian empire and the countries of Syria was al-Malik al-Muzaffar Rokn ad-Din Baibars; the kings, the governors of the provinces, the viziers, were in the same status as the preceding year. This year, the two emirs Ahmad and Talha, sons of Amira, of the tribe of Fadhl, arrived in Cairo; these personages were the sons of the uncle of Hosam ad-Din Mohanna; they had spent a certain time in the empire of the Tatars; they had caused the greatest damage to the Muslims by intercepting the roads. When their father died, they returned under the obedience of the Sultan of Egypt, and the pardon for the crimes they had committed was obtained from this prince in their favor, as well as the concession of fiefs (in the country of Syria); the number of Arabs they commanded amounted to about five thousand tents. This same year, the Shaikh Taki ad-Din ibn at-Taymiyya was sent from Cairo to the maritime place of Alexandria, and no one from his entourage was allowed to leave with him; he entered the city through the wicket gate and was made to descend into the Sultan's palace; then, one night, he was transferred to a tower that is to the west of the city. May Allah grant him the grace of leading him to salvation!
In this year, the Emir 'Ala ad-Din at-Talili and the Emir 'Ala ad-Din al-Khwarizimi arrived; at the same time as them, the Shaikh Abu Yahya Zakaria al-Lihyani arrived, who was viceroy in the name of the prince of Tunis and had the intention of going on the pilgrimage to the holy cities. The two emirs


[p. 147] {#po.20.p147}
ضاحب تونس لقصد الحج وعاد الإميران المذكوران وقد نهب العرب ببلاد المغرب ما كان قد ادسل معهما من البديّة وغيرها ركان فى حملة البدّة من الخيل والبفال والجمال سبع مائة رأن© وفيا :عاد القاضى شم الدين. ابن عدلان من اليمن وان توه اليها فى الرسليّة ومات رفيقه شمس الدين سنقر السعيدى ببلاد اليمن بعد انفصالبما! من الملك المؤتّد صاحب اليمن8
aforementioned returned to the Maghreb (when Abu Yahya Zakaria had left for the Hijaz). The Arabs in the lands of the Maghreb had pillaged the gifts that the King of their country had charged them to bring to Egypt, and all their baggage; among the objects that were addressed to the Sultan of Egypt were seven hundred heads of horses, mules, and camels. This same year, the Qadi Shams ad-Din ibn 'Adlan returned to Cairo from Yemen; he had gone to this country in the capacity of ambassador sent by the Sultan of Egypt. His colleague, Shams ad-Din Sonkor as-Sa'idi, died in Yemen after they had taken leave of al-Malik al-Mu'ayyad, sovereign of this country.

### Account of the history of the emirs who
ذكر من تمر من الامرآ الى السلطان الملك الناصن بالكرك المحروس» قال المؤتيخ ممع اجتمعوا جماعة من الامرآء واتّفقوا على التوجّه الى حهة الكرك وكان خروجيم من القاهرة ليلة الاربحة بعد اذان المغرب خامس عش جمادى الاخر ووصلوا الى الكرك يوم الارعة ثامن وعشرين منه وقيل وصل اوائليم فى جادى وعشرين منهه وهم الامير سيف الدين طقطاى امير مجلس «لامير سيف الدين نفية والامير سيف
fled from Egypt to go find the Sultan al-Malik an-Nasir at al-Karak. The historian said: Then, several emirs assembled and agreed, by unanimous accord, to go to al-Karak. These officers left Cairo during the night of Wednesday, after the call to the sunset prayer, the fifteenth day of the month of Jumada al-Thani; they arrived at al-Karak on Wednesday, the twenty-eighth day of this same month; it has been said that the first arrived on the twenty-first of this month. They were the Emir Saif ad-Din Tokhtai, Emir Madjlis; the Emir Saif ad-Din Noghia; the Emir Saif ad-Din Kiftchak; the Emir 'Ala ad-Din Mogholtai; they


[p. 148] {#po.20.p148}
الدين قفجق والامير علاء الدين مغلطاى وصحبتهم من المماليك! السلطانّة ارسين نفر فكانت عد من قتمز تقدير. تسعين نفر ثم انهم حرّضوا السلطان الملك الناصر على العود الى السلطنة واخبروه انَّ الامرآء معه والدولة بين يديه وانه مال اليهم واتا الملاك المظقر. بيبريئ فاه جزد عسكر فى طلب الامرة المنكودين واخرج السشكر بعد المغرب© ذكر ما حرايات بالثام المحروس© قال المؤتيخ وفى عشيّة يوم اليس سلخ جمادى الاخر وصل الى دمشق عثمان النجّاب وصحته ثلاث مماليك وكتاب من عند السلطان الملك الناصر يطلب من الامرآة الاين المساعدة والمعاضدة وحضوره الى دمشق وان جميع مرا مص معه فى الباطن وعند ذلك جمع ملك الامرآء الامرآ” عنده بالقصر وقرأ عليهم الكتاب© وضريوا مشور وكتبوا له جواب كتابه وهم يقولون إن كان المصربّين
were accompanied by forty Mamluks of the private service of the court; these personages were in all, approximately, ninety; they pressed the Sultan al-Malik an-Nasir to return to take the reins of government in hand; they informed him that the generals were all for him, and that he only had to make a gesture to retake the sovereign power. The prince showed them all the gratitude he felt for their step. As for al-Malik al-Muzaffar Baibars, he had dispatched an army to go catch up with the emirs, and he had made these troops leave after night had fallen. Account of the events that occurred in Damascus, the well-fortified city. The historian said: In the evening of Thursday, the last day of the month of Jumada al-Thani, 'Othman an-Nadjdjab arrived in Damascus accompanied by three Mamluks; he brought a letter that emanated from the Sultan al-Malik an-Nasir, by which this prince asked the emirs of Damascus for help and assistance: he begged them to let him come to Damascus, telling them that all the emirs of Egypt intimately desired that he remount the throne. Upon receipt of this missive, the King of the emirs summoned the generals around his person in the al-Kasr al-ablak, and he read it to them. These officers held council, and they wrote to the Sultan al-Malik an-Nasir


[p. 149] {#po.20.p149}
معك فنحن فى خدمتك ولا فلا تخوض فى دما المسلمين فما لنا نحن قرة بصاحب مص ونحن تبع ليم مع سيّروا الامير علاء الدين ايدغدى شقير وسيف الدين جوبان وابن درباس© وفى شهر رجب الفرد حصل بدمشق خاط واراجيف وانتقل بعض الساكنين بظاهر أللد الى داخلبا واستفاض الحديث ان الملك الناصر خرج من الكرك بمن معه واجتمع امراء دمشق عند النائب مات للمشور ممع وصل بريد من مص وفيه ان الامور على ما هى عليه وانما تخرجت هذه الشرذمة. اليسيرة عن الطاعةه وتحدّث الناس فى تلك الليلة بتوجّه نائب الشام وانتزاحه عن دمشق الى الديار المصريّة ليكون مع الجعّ الففير وكثر خوف الناس فلا اصبحوا آنخر فتح ابواب دمشق الى ان ارتفع النهار واجتمع الناس والامرآء بالقصر وحضروا القضاة وجدّدت الايمان لضاحب مصر الملك المظن دانم
a letter in response to the one he had addressed to them; they said to him: "If the Egyptian generals are with you, we are ready to obey your orders; otherwise, do not plunge yourself into the blood of the Muslims, for we are not strong enough to fight against the Sultan of Egypt, and we will imitate in every point the conduct they will hold toward you." They sent him the emirs 'Ala ad-Din Aidoghdi Shoukaïr, Saif ad-Din Choban, and Ibn Darbas. In the month of Rajab al-Fard, panic and terror reigned in Damascus; a certain number of people who lived in the vicinity of the city transported themselves within its enclosure, and the news spread that al-Malik an-Nasir had left Karak in the company of the persons who had gone there with him. The emirs of Damascus assembled several times at the governor of the place's home to discuss the conduct that should be held. Then, a communiqué arrived from Egypt by post, in which it was said that the situation was exactly as it was exposed there, and that there was only this small number of individuals who had escaped the obedience they owed to the Sultan. During that night, the people recounted that the governor had left Damascus and that he had gone to Egypt to go seek numerous troops; the terror of the inhabitants increased; finally, the next day, at dawn, the gates of Damascus were opened, until the moment when the day was entirely
raised. The people gathered, as did the emirs, in the Kasr al-Ablak; the kadis attended the meeting; the Sultan of Egypt, al-Malik al-Muzaffar, was made to swear an oath again, and they promised to remain faithful to him.


[p. 150] {#po.20.p150}
باقون على طاعته8 ع نودى بدمشق سلطاتكم الملك المظقر طتبوا قلونكم: ومن تكلم فيما لا يضيه قولى على ذلك نمع حضر حندى من غلمان الملك الناصن واخين تيوصوله الى اذوعات وظهر من ناب السلطنة الحد فى مقاتاته وانّه لا يمكنه من البلد© وكان قد شيّر اليه الامير علاء الدين ايدغدى شقير والامير سيف الدين جوبان حتى يرجّعوا عن المجىء فسادوا الاميرين من عند الملك الناضر وقد خلع عليهم واعطا لكل واحد منهم القت دثار وقال انا ولدكم وانتم رتيتمونى ولا ب لى من المجىء اليكم فمن شاه يحارنى ومن شاه يسالمنى© فئد ذلك لان نائب السسلطنة خوفا على نفنه لانّه ظبن له ان ما معه أحاد يقائل وان الصسكر قد مالى اكثرة الى الماك الناصر ثم سيّر نائب_ السلطنة الاميرين سيف الدين بيادر اص وسيفه الدين بكُتسر -الحاجب ليلة: الانثينئ الى الملك الناصس يشير
Then, it was proclaimed in Damascus: "Your sultan is al-Malik al-Muzaffar; calm your minds; as for anyone who speaks of things that do not concern him, he will have to account for his words!" In the meantime, a horseman arrived who was one of the orderlies of al-Malik an-Nasir, and he brought the crowd the news that his master was arriving by forced marches. The viceroy of Damascus showed sentiments indicating that he would do everything possible to fight al-Malik an-Nasir, and that he would not let him seize the city. He had already dispatched the emirs 'Ala ad-Din Aydoghdi Shukayr and Sayf ad-Din Choban to him to dissuade him from coming to Damascus; these two emirs returned after having an interview with al-Malik an-Nasir; the prince decorated them with robes of honor, and he gave each of them a sum of one thousand dinars; he said to them: "I am your son, and it is you who raised me; I absolutely must go to you; whoever wants to will make war on me; whoever wants to will treat me as a friend!" It was at this moment that the viceroy of Damascus abandoned his bellicose dispositions, for fear that some misfortune might befall him, because he was well persuaded that not one of his soldiers wanted to fight and that the great majority of his troops desired the sovereignty of al-Malik an-Nasir. However, after this realization, the viceroy sent off the two emirs Sayf ad-Din Bahadur As and Sayf ad-Din Bektumur, the chamberlain, during the night of Monday, with the mission of going to al-Malik an-Nasir to advise him to return to al-Karak, and to inform him that the army


[p. 151] {#po.20.p151}
عليه بالرجوع ونخر بان حستكي ذعشق غير مطبعين له ولا يتسترر قتليم للمصرين بوه من الوجوه» فرصا الى دمشق ليلة الثلااء وانضرا ان الملك الشاصر لم يوجد فى المنزلة التى كان نا بها وإنه ررحم الى التكرك ولم يتحقق سيب رجوعه واصبع الناس .يوم الثلانة خاسى الشهر فى سكون عظيع بسنب عوذتة© ونقك نائب السلطئنة الى القضن بعض ما كان دخل بة الى اللد وكذلك بعض الأمرا والعتّام ونشات فى هذ الإتّام الغرارة القمح: من الصالحية باربعة ذزاهم ومن العرة بخعسة دراهم نع سكن حا الناس وام تبث خواطرهم» فلعًا كان يوم الخميس ثالث عش شعبان كثر الربمج بدمشق ونا باسم السلطان الملك الناسسي بين عاتة الناس وات واصلًا وانّ الامييين سيف الدين تطاونك وسيف الدين الاج بادر قصداة ولحقا به وكان قد فز اليه الامير ركن الدين ببيبرى العجنون ويكن الدين بيبرسن العلمى مع أنَ نائب الملطنة بالغام المحروس ارسل الامير علم الدين
of Damascus was in no way disposed to receive his orders, and that he should not imagine that it was going to engage in a war with the troops of the Sultan of Egypt, under any pretext whatsoever. During the night of Tuesday, the two emirs returned, and they informed the governor that al-Malik an-Nasir had not been at the stage where he had established his camp, and that he had returned to al-Karak, without it being possible to discern the reasons that had incited him to renounce his march on the capital. The inhabitants, at the dawn of this Tuesday, the fifth day of the month, felt great peace of mind, as a result of the return of al-Malik an-Nasir to his fortress. The governor transferred to the Kasr a portion of the people who had come to seek refuge with his person in the city, as well as several emirs and soldiers. In those days, the ghirara of wheat coming from as-Salihiyya reached the rate of four dirhams, and that coming from the Mizza rose to the price of five dirhams. Then, the people calmed down, and they regained the tranquility of their minds. When Thursday, the thirteenth day of the month of Sha'ban, arrived, the revolution broke out in Damascus, and the name of the sultan al-Malik an-Nasir was proclaimed before the populace, that this prince was arriving, that the two emirs Sayf ad-Din Qutlughbeg and Sayf ad-Din al-Hajj Bahadur had left to meet him, and
had joined him; furthermore, the emirs Rukn ad-Din Baybars al-Majnun and Rukn ad-Din Baybars al-'Alami had fled from Damascus to go and place themselves


[p. 152] {#po.20.p152}
ألجاولى والامير عر الدين الزردكاش وعلاء الدين ايدغدى الجمالى الى السلطان الملك الناصر لاصلاح امرنا والاعتذار عنهه ووصل الى دمشق الامير سيف الدين بكتمر الجوكندار نائب السلطنة بصفد فى نصف شعان ثعّ انّ نائب السلطنة بدمشق كثر قلقه وفكر فى املا ورأى لنفسه الانتزاح عن البلد وصتم على ذلك وتوجّه فى ليلة الاحد سادس عش شعبان هو وخواصه على البجن والخيل وسلكوا المرّةٍ الى البقاع ومعه ابن صبح مقدم الجيلتّة ثم الى شقيف ارنون وخلا القصر الابلق بحيث لم يبق فيه
in his service. Then, the viceroy, governor of Damascus, the well-guarded city, sent off the emir 'Alam ad-Din Altunbugha, the emirs 'Izz ad-Din az-Zaradkash and 'Ala ad-Din Aydoghdi al-Jamali, and he sent them to al-Malik an-Nasir to save his situation, and to beg him to excuse the conduct he had been forced to maintain. The emir Sayf ad-Din Bektumur al-Jukandar, governor of Safad, arrived in Damascus on the fifteenth day of the month of Sha'ban. The viceroy, governor of Damascus, felt his anxieties redouble; after having reflected well on the gravity of his position, he saw no other chance of salvation for himself than to move away from the capital, and this idea never left him. Thus, during the night of Sunday, the fifteenth day of the month of Sha'ban, this officer and his faithful followers left the city, mounted on camels and horses. They followed the Mizza until they arrived at the Biqa'; the viceroy had with him ('Alam ad-Din) ibn Subh, chief of the men who inhabit the mountain; from there, they reached Shaqif Arnon. The Kasr al-Ablak had been
entirely emptied, to the point that not a piece of furniture or a utensil remained. It is said that everything had been looted by the crowd; the situation of the viceroy and his partisans had (as a result) become singularly precarious.


[p. 153] {#po.20.p153}
شىء من الاثاث والالات وقيل انها نبيت واضطرب امن نائب السلطنة واتاعه» ذكر وصول السلطان الملك الناصر الى الشام المحروس© قال المؤتّخ نمع نهض الامير ركن الدين بيبرس العلاثى والامير سيف الدين اجا والامير جمال الدين الطشلاقى وعملوا ما يليق بدست الملك من الكوسات والعصائب والجتر وغير ذلك وسّروهم الى السلطان» وفى عشيّة يوم الاحد وصل علاء الدين ايدغدى الجمالى والزردكاش بأمان من حهة السلطان ألملك الناص لنائب السلطتة فلم يجدوه فتوجّها نحو ليخرالا بذلك وخرجوا الامراآء للقاء السلطان الملك الناصر ودعى له على المنابر .بجامع دمشق ليلة الاثنين سابع عشر شعانة ونودى فى اقل ليلة الثلثا” ثامن عشر»ب فى البلد عن امرلا بفتح الدكاكين والشروع فى الزيئة ودثت البشائر بالقلعة واستمتر ذلك عشرة ايام مع خرج الاكابر والقضاة وكان وصول السلطان الملك الناصر الى دمشق فى الساعة السابعة من .يوم الثلثاة ثامن عشر شعان سنة
Account of the arrival of the sultan al-Malik an-Nasir in Damascus, the well-guarded. The historian said: Then, the emir Rukn ad-Din Baybars al-'Alayi, the emir Sayf ad-Din Aqchebe, and the emir Sayf ad-Din at-Tashlaki hastened to prepare everything required for the imperial pomp—drums, standards, parasol, and other similar insignia—which they sent to the sultan. In the evening of Sunday, 'Ala ad-Din Aydoghdi al-Jamali and 'Izz ad-Din az-Zaradkash arrived; these two officers brought a letter by which the sultan al-Malik an-Nasir granted a full pardon to the viceroy; these officers did not find him in the city, and they set out to go and inform him of the favor he was the object of. The emirs left Damascus to go and pay their respects to the sultan al-Malik an-Nasir, and prayer was said in the name of this prince from the pulpits in the Great Mosque of Damascus during the night of Monday, the seventeenth of the month of Sha'ban. In the early hours of Tuesday, the eighteenth day of this same month, it was proclaimed in the city, on the order of the prince, that the merchants were to reopen their shops, and that the inhabitants were to do their best to decorate. The drums were beaten in the citadel, and the festivities continued for ten days; the great dignitaries of the state and the kadis left in their turn to go and receive the sultan al-Malik an-Nasir, who made his entry into Damascus at the seventh hour of Tuesday, the eighteenth day of the month of Sha'ban of the year 709, the astrological ascendant being the last degrees of the zodiacal sign of Taurus and the first of Gemini.


[p. 154] {#po.20.p154}
تسع وسبعشة والطالع آخر الثور وال الجوزاء» فلا وصل الى باب القلعة لم ينزل بها ونزل بالقضر الابلق وكان الامير سيف الدين الحاج ادر خامل الجتر .وغليه خلعة عغليمة منحجّة واقكى من "حمل القامية الامير حيف الدين تطلوبك الكبير ومن بعد بنحبّة الامرآء واحد بعد واحه» وعند نزول السلطان بالحيدان الاخضر! احضر له الامير سنيف الدين يادر المنجرى متولى قلعة ددهق سماطً عظيماً ولم يملع منه أحد من السبكنى والعام وخلم العلطان على السنجري© وفى آنخر انان وضلك الامين سيفت الدين نمس التناقى نائب حعض وفى يوم الاربطة تتاسع عش الشهز حلفوا عسكر صفد وستقروهم الى غئةِ وقتموا غليهم الامير سيف الدين اقفتا الظاهرى وميف الدين تمر بحيث يكونوا
When the sultan had reached the gate of the citadel, he did not wish to descend there, and (he decided to go) to settle in the Kasr al-Ablak; the emir Sayf ad-Din al-Hajj Bahadur carried the parasol above the head of the sovereign; he was dressed in the great robe of honor, all embroidered with gold; the first general who carried the ghashiya before al-Malik an-Nasir was the emir Sayf ad-Din Qutlughbeg, the great emir; after this dignitary, all the generals carried this insignia, one after the other, in turn. At the moment when the Sultan of Egypt arrived at the Green Hippodrome, the emir Sayf ad-Din Bahadur as-Sinjari, governor of the citadel of Damascus, begged him to honor a sumptuous feast with his presence, and no one, neither soldier nor civilian, was prevented from coming to satisfy their hunger there. The sultan decorated as-Sinjari with a robe of honor. At the end of the day, the emir Sayf ad-Din Temur as-Saqi, governor of the fortress of Homs, arrived; on Wednesday, the nineteenth day of the month, the army of Safad was made to swear an oath, and these troops were sent to Gaza, after having given them as commanders the emirs Sayf ad-Din Aqchebe az-Zahiri and Sayf ad-Din Temur, so that they might provide cover against the
Egyptians and prevent the soldiers who wanted to pass from Syria to Egypt from accomplishing their design.


[p. 155] {#po.20.p155}
برك بسب المضرتّين ومن يقتمز من الشامه وست السلطان امانا لملك الامراة مرّة اخرى وفى يوم السبت ثانى وعشرين شعبان وصل ملك الامراء الامير خمال الدين اقوش الافرم مذعنًا بالطاعة والشتقاه السلطان خارج العيدان وقتموا. للسلطان امير موسى ولد الافرم فباس الارض ثم دفعون الى السلطان فاسه ثلاث مات ثم حضى ابول بعده فقتّك الارض فعند ذلك ترجّل له السلطان وعانقه وباس رأسه© ورب السلطان ومشى فى دكابه الى "القص فنزل السلكان وعضدة الافرم وحصل من السلطان ممائبة لطيفة وفى يوم الاثنين دابع وعشرين منه ' وصلى الامين سيف الدين قتحق نائب حماةٌ والامير سقفت الدين اسندمر نائب طرابلس وتلقّاهم السلطان والامراء كما تلقوا الافرم8 واقيمت صلاة الجمعة ثامن وعشرين الشهى بالميدان وحمل. الى هناك المنبر وسناحتق الخطيب وعد الصلاة
The sultan sent a letter a second time to the king of the emirs by which he declared that he granted him an absolute pardon. On Saturday, the twenty-second day of the said month of Sha'ban, the king of the emirs, the emir Jamal ad-Din Ughush al-Afram, arrived to make his submission to al-Malik an-Nasir; the sultan met him outside the Green Hippodrome; the emir Musa, son of al-Afram, was presented to the prince; this personage kissed the earth; he was raised up, and he was made to advance to the sultan, who embraced him three times; then, his father presented himself and kissed the earth; al-Malik an-Nasir immediately descended from his steed to show him the esteem in which he held him, he pressed him in his arms, and kissed him on the head. The sultan got back into the saddle and marched at the head of his procession to the Kasr al-Ablak; he dismounted, and al-Afram held his stirrup to help him. The prince then made him some slight reproaches (regarding the resistance he had opposed to him when he was at Karak). On Monday, the twenty-fourth day of Sha'ban, the emirs Sayf ad-Din Qipchaq, governor of Hama, and Sayf ad-Din Esendemur, governor of Tripoli, arrived; the sultan and the emirs welcomed them with as much eagerness as they had shown in receiving al-Afram. The great Friday prayer was held on the twenty-eighth day of the month in
the Green Hippodrome, where the preaching pulpit (from the Umayyad Mosque) and the preacher's standards had been transported. In the meantime, arrived


[p. 156] {#po.20.p156}
وصل الامير شمس الدين قرا ستقر نائب حلب والتقاد السلطان وترجّل له وتانقا ولا وحمل الغاشية وت بوصوله جميع امن الامراة وطاعتهم© قال المؤتخ ثم نف فى اليش وسفر بعضه الى غرة نمع حض بقّة الامراء الحلبيّين ونزلوا بظاهر دمشق بالجسورة وفى يوم الست سادس ذى القعدةة وصل مملوك من ممليك الاشرف من مص واخبر انّ الملك المظتّر قد حصّن قلعة القاهرة وان سلار بالجيزة عند خبوله وهو متمرّض صورة فان المظتر عمل له سقية وفى ثامن الشهر وصل سسّة نفر مماليك واخيروا انّ ع جماعة من الامرآ منتظرين قدوم السلطان وهم خائفين ان يعلم يم المظتقر فيقبض علييم
the emir Shams ad-Din Qara-Sonqur, governor of Aleppo; the sultan set out on horseback to go and welcome him, and he dismounted as soon as he saw him; the prince and the emir embraced for a long time; Shams ad-Din Qara-Sonqur in his turn carried the ghashiya before al-Malik an-Nasir. With his arrival, all the questions that arose regarding the attitude of the emirs and their recognition of the sultan were definitively resolved.
